Global Maximal Regularity for equations with degenerate weights Thanks: Anna Kh.Balci and Lars Diening thank for the financial support the Deutsche Forschungsgemeinschaft (DFG, German Science Foundation) through SFB 1283/2 2021-317210226 at Bielefeld University. Sun-Sig Byun thanks for the financial support from the National Research Foundation of Korea (NRF) through NRF-2021R1A4A1027378 at Seoul National University, Ho-Sik Lee thanks for the financial support from the National Research Foundation of Korea (NRF) through IRTG 2235/NRF-2016K2A9A2A13003815 at Seoul National University.
Anna Kh. Balci, Sun-Sig Byun, Lars Diening, Ho-Sik Lee

Abstract
. In this paper we are concerned with global maximal regularity estimates for elliptic equations with degenerate weights. We consider both the linear case and the non-linear case. We show that higher integrability of the gradients can be obtained by imposing a local small oscillation condition on the weight and a local small Lipschitz condition on the boundary of the domain. Our results are new in the linear and non-linear case. We show by example that the relation between the exponent of higher integrability and the smallness parameters is sharp even in the linear or the unweighted case.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
35J92, 35J70, 35J25, 35B65, 46E35

1.
Introduction

We study the following degenerate elliptic equation of the form

(1.1)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} -\operatorname{div}(\mathbb{A}(x)\nabla u)&=-\operatorname{div}(\mathbb{A}(x)F)&\qquad&\text{in }\Omega,\\
u&=0&\qquad&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{aligned}

in the linear case, and of the form

(1.2)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} -\operatorname{div}(|\mathbb{M}(x)\nabla u|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}^{2}(x)\nabla u)&=-\operatorname{div}(|\mathbb{M}(x)F|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}^{2}(x)F)&\qquad&\text{in }\Omega,\\
u&=0&\qquad&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{aligned}

in the non-linear case. We often write
{\mathbb{M}}(x)
to emphasize the dependence of the weight on
x
. Here,
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a bounded domain with
n\geq 2
,
1<p<\infty
,
F:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a given vector-valued function,
\mathbb{M}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is a given symmetric and positive definite matrix-valued weight satisfying

(1.3)

\displaystyle{\lvert{\mathbb{M}(x)}\rvert}\,{\lvert{\mathbb{M}^{-1}(x)}\rvert}\leq\Lambda\quad(x\in\mathbb{R}^{n})

for some constant
\Lambda\geq 1
, where
{\lvert{\cdot}\rvert}
is the spectral norm, and
\mathbb{A}(x):=\mathbb{M}^{2}(x)
. This condition says that
{\mathbb{M}}
has a uniformly bounded condition number. Note that a right-hand side of the form
-\operatorname{div}G
with
G\,:\,\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
can be immediately rewritten in the above form in terms of
F
. Note that (
1.1
) is a special case of (
1.2
) for
p=2
. The condition (
1.3
) in this case reads as

(1.4)

\displaystyle{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}\,{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}(x)}\rvert}\leq\Lambda^{2}\quad(x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}).

Let us define the scalar weight

(1.5)

\displaystyle\omega(x)=|\mathbb{M}(x)|=\sqrt{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}}.

Suppose that
\omega^{p}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{p}
-Muckenhoupt weight (see Section 2) and
F\in L^{p}_{\omega}(\Omega):=L^{p}(\Omega,\omega^{p}\,dx)
, then there exists a unique weak solution
u\in W^{1,p}_{0,\omega}(\Omega)
of (
1.2
), which means

(1.6)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{M}(x)\nabla u|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}^{2}(x)\nabla u\cdot\nabla\varphi\,dx=\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{M}(x)F|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}^{2}(x)F\cdot\nabla\varphi\,dx

for all
\varphi\in W^{1,p}_{0,\omega}(\Omega)
. Moreover, we have the following standard energy estimate

(1.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\leq c\int_{\Omega}|F|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx

with
c=c(n,p,\Lambda)
, see
[
BDGN21
,
CMP18
]
.

This paper aims for proving the following global maximal regularity estimates

(1.8)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{q}\omega^{q}\,dx\leq c\int_{\Omega}|F|^{q}\omega^{q}\,dx

for every
q\in(1,\infty)
in the linear case (
1.1
), and for every
q\in[p,\infty)
in the non-linear case (
1.2
). The positive constant
c
is independent of
F
and
u
, under minimal extra assumptions on both the boundary of
\Omega
and the weight
\mathbb{M}
in addition to (
1.3
). We pay special attention to the optimal dependence of the parameters of the boundary and of the coefficients on
q
. Estimates of this type are also known under the name of global
non-linear Calderón-Zygmund estimates
. Our main results are presented in Theorem
1.3
and
1.4
.

We first explain the linear case. When
\mathbb{A}
is the identity matrix, our result is related to
[
CZ52
,
CZ56
]
, from which the linear Calderón-Zygmund theory originates. If
\mathbb{A}(x)
is assumed to be only measurable, but uniformly elliptic in the sense that

(1.9)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}|\xi|^{2}\leq\left<\mathbb{A}(x)\xi,\xi\right>\leq\lambda_{\max}|\xi|^{2}

for any
x\in\Omega
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, a local version of the result (
1.8
) is proved in
[
Mey63
]
for
q\in[2,2+\varepsilon)
for some small
\varepsilon>0
. To obtain the estimate for all
q\in(1,\infty)
, one needs additional regularity assumption on
\mathbb{A}
.
In
[
Di 96
]
the assumption
\mathbb{A}\in{\mathrm{VMO}}
is made to prove (
1.8
) for
q\in(1,\infty)
for
\partial\Omega\in C^{1,1}
and in
[
AQ02
]
for
\partial\Omega\in C^{1}
. A global result on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is obtained in
[
IS98
]
and a local result for the case of systems is proved in
[
DFZ13
]
for
{\mathbb{A}}\in{\mathrm{VMO}}
. The condition
\mathbb{A}\in{\mathrm{VMO}}
is relaxed to a small
{\mathrm{BMO}}
condition. The global results for bounded domains are obtained in a series of papers
[
Byu05
,
BCKW07
,
BW04
]
.

In this paper we are also interested in the degenerate case, where (
1.9
) fails. The most simple example of which is
{\mathbb{A}}(x)=|x|^{\pm\varepsilon}\mathrm{Id}
with
\varepsilon>0
small. Instead of (
1.9
), we assume

(1.10)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-2}\mu(x)|\xi|^{2}\leq\left<\mathbb{A}(x)\xi,\xi\right>\leq\mu(x)|\xi|^{2}

where
\mu(x):={\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}={\lvert{{\mathbb{M}}(x)}\rvert}^{2}=\omega^{2}(x)
. In
[
FKS82
]
, it is proved that if
\mu
belongs to the Muckenhoupt class
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, then the solution
u
of (
1.1
) is Hölder continuous. Gradient estimates are obtained in
[
CMP18
]
under (
1.10
),
\mu\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
and a smallness assumption in terms of a weighted
{\mathrm{BMO}}
norm of
\mathbb{A}
. They yield
|F|^{q}\mu\in L^{1}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}\Rightarrow|\nabla u|^{q}\mu\in L^{1}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}
for all
q\in(1,\infty)
, including the case
\mu(x)=|x|^{\pm\varepsilon}\mathrm{Id}
for small
\varepsilon>0
. The global result is obtained in
[
Pha20
]
and the local result for the case of systems is proved in
[
CMP19
]
. In the recent paper
[
BDGN21
]
, the authors prove a new type of gradient estimates with the implication that
(|F|\omega)^{q}\in L^{1}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}\Rightarrow(|\nabla u|\omega)^{q}\in L^{1}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}
for all
q\in(1,\infty)
, assuming (
1.10
) and the smallness condition for the
{\mathrm{BMO}}
norm of
\log\mathbb{A}
as follows:

(1.11)

\displaystyle\sup_{B\Subset\Omega}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\lvert\log\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\log\mathbb{A}}\rangle_{B}|\,dx\leq\frac{\delta}{q}

for some
\delta=\delta(n,p,\Lambda)
, where
\langle{f}\rangle_{B}=\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}f\,dx
for an integrable function
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{{\mathrm{sym}}}
. Here, we can define
\log\mathbb{A}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{{\mathrm{sym}}}
, the logarithm of the matrix-valued weight
\mathbb{A}
, since
\mathbb{A}
is positive definite almost everywhere. This novel
\log
–
{\mathrm{BMO}}
condition of
[
BDGN21
]
not only includes the degenerate weights of the form
\mathbb{A}(x)=|x|^{\pm\varepsilon}\mathrm{Id}
for small
\varepsilon>0
, but also has the optimality in terms of the obtainable integrability exponent
q
. Compared to
[
CMP18
]
, where
\mu\,dx
is treated as a measure, the degenerate weight
\mu
or better
\omega
in
[
BDGN21
]
plays the role of a multiplier.
Also in this paper we treat
\omega
as a multiplier, which seems also important for the optimal dependency of
q
on the constants.
We compare the conditions used in
[
CMP18
]
and those introduced in
[
BDGN21
]
in Section
4.2
.

Now, consider the
p
-Laplacian case. If we write

A(\xi):=|\xi|^{p-2}\xi
and
\mathcal{A}(x,\xi):=|\mathbb{M}(x)\xi|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}^{2}(x)\xi
, then (
1.2
) is equivalent to

(1.12)

\displaystyle-\operatorname{div}\mathcal{A}(\cdot,\nabla u)=-\operatorname{div}\mathcal{A}(\cdot,F).

Writing
\mathbb{M}^{2}=\mathbb{A}
and
\mathcal{A}(\cdot,F)=G
for
\mathbb{A}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{{\mathrm{sym}}}
and
G\,:\,\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
we can write (
1.12
) as

(1.13)

\displaystyle-\operatorname{div}\left(\left<\mathbb{A}\nabla u,\nabla u\right>^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\mathbb{A}\nabla u\right)=-\operatorname{div}G.

Then
u
is the minimizer of the following functional:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(v)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{p}\int_{\Omega}\left<\mathbb{A}\nabla v,\nabla v\right>^{\frac{p}{2}}\,dx-\int_{\Omega}G\cdot\nabla v\,dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{M}\nabla v|^{p}\,dx-\int_{\Omega}|\mathbb{M}F|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}F\cdot(\mathbb{M}\nabla v)\,dx.

If
p\in(1,\infty)
and
\mathbb{M}=\mathrm{Id}
, then
A(\nabla u)=\mathcal{A}(\cdot,\nabla u)
. In this case, the Hölder continuity of
u
and
\nabla u
is investigated in
[
Lew83
,
Uhl77
]
, and the gradient regularity estimates were obtained in
[
DM93
,
Iwa83
]
. In the recent years, there have been many research activities for the gradient estimates in terms of
A(\nabla u)
. The
{\mathrm{BMO}}
type estimate with the implication that
G\in{\mathrm{BMO}}\Rightarrow A(\nabla u)\in{\mathrm{BMO}}
is shown in
[
DM93
]
for
p>2
and
[
DKS12
]
for
1<p<\infty
. In
[
DKS12
]
, the implication
G\in C^{0,\alpha}\Rightarrow A(\nabla u)\in C^{0,\alpha}
for small
\alpha>0
is proved. A local pointwise estimate is proved in
[
BCDKS18
]
and extended to the global one in
[
BCDS22
]
. Estimates in Besov space and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces up to differentiability one for
n=2
and
p>2
are shown in
[
BDW20
]
. Besov space regularity for
\nabla u
is also considered in
[
BCGOP17
,
CGP19
]
. The result
A(\nabla u)\in W^{1,2}
when
\operatorname{div}G\in L^{2}
is obtained in
[
CM18
]
for scalar equations for
p>1
and for vectorial systems in
[
CM19
]
for
p>\frac{3}{2}
and for
p>2(2-\sqrt{2})\approx 1.1715
in
[
BCDM21
]
. Gradient potential estimates are studied for equations in
[
KM12
,
KM13
]
and for systems in
[
BY19
,
DM10
,
KM14
,
KM18
]
.

Now, we pay attention to the weighted case. The local version of (
1.8
) is proved for
1<p<\infty
, with a uniformly elliptic weight
\mathbb{M}
as in (
1.9
) with
\mathbb{M}\in{\mathrm{VMO}}
in
[
KZ99
]
. Since
\mathbb{M}
is uniformly elliptic, we have
\omega(x)\eqsim 1
, so the results reduce to the transfer of
L^{q}
-regularity from
F
to
\nabla u
. The global estimate is obtained in
[
KZ01
]
with a
C^{1,\alpha}
domain
\Omega
for
\alpha\in(0,1]
. The assumption
\mathbb{M}\in{\mathrm{VMO}}
has been weakened to the one that
\mathbb{M}
has a small
{\mathrm{BMO}}
-norm, as shown in
[
BW07
,
BWZ07
,
Cho18
,
LCZ18
]
. Under similar assumptions it is possible to replace the
L^{q}
-regularity transfer by
L^{q}(\sigma\,dx)
-regularity transfer for suitable Muckenhoupt weights
\sigma
, see
[
BR13
,
MP11
,
MP12
,
Phu11
]
. Note that the weight
\sigma
is not related to the weight
\omega
of the equation.

Now, we introduce Lipschitz domains along with our optimal regularity assumption for the boundary of the domain.

Definition 1.1
.

Let
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2n}]
and
R>0
be given. Then
\Omega
is called
(\delta,R)
–Lipschitz if for each
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
, there exists a coordinate system
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}
and Lipschitz map
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
x_{0}=0
in this coordinate system, and there holds

(1.14)

\displaystyle\Omega\cap B_{R}(x_{0})=\{x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=(x^{\prime},x_{n})\in B_{R}(x_{0}):x_{n}>\psi(x^{\prime})\}

and

(1.15)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}\leq\delta.

Imposing a Lipschitz condition for the boundary of the domain appears in many papers, the regularity and the asymptotic behavior of caloric function
[
ACS96
]
, homogenization
[
KS11
]
, oblique derivative problem
[
Lie87
,
Lie88a
,
Lie02
]
, Hölder continuity of solutions for Robin boundary condition
[
Nit11
]
, regularity results for elliptic Dirichlet problem
[
Sav98
]
, Calderón-Zygmund estimates
[
Byu05
,
BW07
]
. We would like to point out that in
[
KZ01
]

C^{1,\alpha}
regularity with
\alpha\in(0,1]
is assumed for
\partial\Omega
. It was observed in
[
AQ02
]
(in the linear case) that
\partial\Omega\in C^{1}
is enough.
Our Lipschitz assumption for the boundary is weaker than both
C^{1,\alpha}
and
C^{1}
assumption on
\partial\Omega
, so Theorem
1.3
and
1.4
can be both applied in particular to
C^{1,\alpha}
and
C^{1}
-domains. Our assumption is indeed an optimal one to be discussed in Section
4.1
. The sharp relation between the smallness parameter of the boundary and the integrability exponent
q
is,
as far as we know, new in the literature, even in the unweighted, linear case.

Our optimal regularity assumption for
\mathbb{M}
is a small
{\mathrm{BMO}}
assumption on its logarithm. This condition is also used in
[
BDGN21
]
for the interior estimates.

Definition 1.2
.

We say that
\log\mathbb{M}
is
(\delta,R)
-vanishing if

(1.16)

\displaystyle\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}:=\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sup_{0<r\leq R}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{r}(y)}\lvert\log\mathbb{M}(x)-\langle{\log\mathbb{M}}\rangle_{B_{r}(y)}|\,dx\leq\delta.

Now, we state the main theorems.

Theorem 1.3

(Linear case)
.

Define
\omega
as (
1.5
), and assume (
1.3
) and
F\in L^{q}_{\omega}(\Omega)
for
q\in(1,\infty)
in (
1.1
). Then there exists a constant
\delta=\delta(n,\Lambda)\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
such that if for some
R
,

(1.17a)

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{A}\,\,\,\text{is}\,\,\,\left(\delta\min\left\{\frac{1}{q},1-\frac{1}{q}\right\},R\right)\text{--vanishing and}

(1.17b)

\displaystyle\Omega\,\,\text{is}\,\,\left(\delta\min\left\{\frac{1}{q},1-\frac{1}{q}\right\},R\right)\text{--Lipschitz},

then the weak solution
u\in W^{1,2}_{0,\omega}(\Omega)
of (
1.1
) satisfies
\nabla u\in L^{q}_{\omega}(\Omega)
and we have the estimate

(1.18)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla u|\omega)^{q}\,dx\leq c\int_{\Omega}(|F|\omega)^{q}\,dx

for some
c=c(n,\Lambda,\Omega,q)
.

For the non-linear case, we have the following result.

Theorem 1.4

(Non-linear case)
.

Define
\omega
as (
1.5
), and assume (
1.3
) and
F\in L^{q}_{\omega}(\Omega)
for
q\in[p,\infty)
in (
1.2
). Then there exists a constant
\delta=\delta(n,p,\Lambda)\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
such that
if for some
R
,

(1.19)

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{M}\,\,\,\text{is}\,\,\,\left(\dfrac{\delta}{q},R\right)\text{--vanishing and }\Omega\,\,\text{is}\,\,\left(\dfrac{\delta}{q},R\right)\text{--Lipschitz},

then the weak solution
u\in W^{1,p}_{0,\omega}(\Omega)
of (
1.2
) satisfies
\nabla u\in L^{q}_{\omega}(\Omega)
and we have the estimate

(1.20)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla u|\omega)^{q}\,dx\leq c\int_{\Omega}(|F|\omega)^{q}\,dx

for some
c=c(n,p,\Lambda,\Omega,q)
.

In principle we use a standard perturbation argument combined with the regularity of
p
-harmonic functions. This argument is for example developed in
[
Iwa82
]
and
[
CP98
]
, and used in
[
KZ01
]
. However, we modify this technique such that it is possible to obtain optimal estimates in terms of the smallness of oscillation parameter
\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}}
and the boundary regularity parameter
\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}
. In particular, we obtain a linear dependence for the reciprocal of the integrability exponent instead of an exponential one. This is one of the main novelties of this paper.

The approach from
[
Iwa82
]
and
[
CP98
]
can be reduced to redistributional estimates in terms of maximal operator of the gradient. However this technique always introduces an exponential dependence of
q
on the smallness parameter
\delta
. We avoid this problem by using a qualitative version of the global Fefferman-Stein inequality
\|f\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq cq\|\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}_{1}f\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
(see (
3.97
) in Section 3). The important feature is the linear dependency on the exponent
q
. This allows us to extract the sharp dependency of
\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}}
and
\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}
.

The interior maximal regularity with optimal constants was already described in
[
BDGN21
]
. In this paper we extend those results up to the boundary with an optimal dependence and the boundary parameters. To this end, we first use the localization argument adapted to our boundary comparison estimate and provide the pointwise sharp maximal function estimate for the localized function of
u
. As an auxiliary step we provide
C^{1,\alpha}
-regularity and the decay estimates up to boundary for the solutions of the reference problems. To this end, we employ the reflection principle of the reference problems, which is one of the intrinsic property in the divergence type equation, see
[
Mar81
]
.

This paper is organized as follows: Section 2 is devoted to the basic notation and results which will be frequently used throughout the paper. In Section 3 we provide the proof of Theorem
1.3
and Theorem
1.4
. In Section 4 we discuss the sharpness of our smallness assumptions (
1.17a
) and (
1.19
).

2.
Notation and Preliminary Results

Denote
B_{r}(x)\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
by the open ball of radius
r>0
and center
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For a ball
B
, let
r_{B}
be the radius and
x_{B}
be the center of
B
. For
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
write
B_{r}^{+}(x)=B_{r}(x)\cap\{y=(y_{1},\dots,y_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:y_{n}\geq x_{n}\}
. For an open set
U
and a function
f
we abbreviate
\langle{f}\rangle_{U}:=\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{U}f(x)\,dx
. We write
\chi_{U}
for the characteristic function of the set
U
. Let us denote
c
by a general constant depending only on
n,p
and
\Lambda
which may vary on different occasions. We also write
f\lesssim g
when
f\leq cg
, and write
f\eqsim g
when
f\lesssim g
and
g\lesssim f
hold. For
1<p<\infty
,
p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{p-1}
means the conjugate exponent of
p
.

2.1.
Matrix-valued Weights and Logarithms

We write
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{{\mathrm{sym}}}
for symmetric, real-valued matrices. We denote
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{\geq 0}
by the cone of symmetric, real-valued and positive semidefinite matrices. The collection of positive definite matrices is denoted by
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}
. For
\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{{\mathrm{sym}}}
, we write
\mathbb{X}\geq\mathbb{Y}
provided
\mathbb{X}-\mathbb{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{\geq 0}
.

Let
\mathbb{M}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{\geq 0}
be a (matrix-valued) weight if
\mathbb{M}
is positive definite a.e., and
\omega\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
be a (scalar) weight if
\omega
is positive a.e.. For
\mathbb{L}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, let
|\mathbb{L}|
denote the spectral norm, which means
|\mathbb{L}|=\sup_{|\xi|\leq 1}|\mathbb{L}\xi|
. If
{\mathbb{L}}
is symmetric, then
{\lvert{{\mathbb{L}}}\rvert}=\sup_{{\lvert{\xi}\rvert}\leq 1}{\langle{{\mathbb{L}}\xi},{\xi}\rangle}
.

We consider the matrix exponential
\exp\colon\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{{\mathrm{sym}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}
, with its unique inverse mapping
\log\colon\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{{\mathrm{sym}}}
. Thus, we can define
\log\mathbb{M}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{{\mathrm{sym}}}
, since
\mathbb{M}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{{\mathrm{sym}}}
is positive definite a.e. We now define the logarithmic means

\displaystyle\langle{\omega}\rangle^{\log}_{U}

\displaystyle:=\exp(\langle{\log\omega}\rangle_{U}),

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbb{M}}\rangle^{\log}_{U}

\displaystyle:=\exp(\langle{\log\mathbb{M}}\rangle_{U})

for some subset
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The logarithmic mean has the following compatibility property under taking reciprocal:

\displaystyle\left<\dfrac{1}{\omega}\right>^{\log}_{U}=\exp(-\langle{\log\omega}\rangle_{U})=\dfrac{1}{\langle{\omega}\rangle^{\log}_{U}}.

Moreover, using the relations
\log(\mathbb{M}^{-1})=-\log\mathbb{M}
and
(\exp(\mathbb{L}))^{-1}=\exp(-\mathbb{L})
, we also obtain

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbb{M}^{-1}}\rangle^{\log}_{U}=\exp(-\langle{\log(\mathbb{M})}\rangle_{U})=\left(\exp(\langle{\log(\mathbb{M})}\rangle_{U})\right)^{-1}=(\langle{\mathbb{M}}\rangle^{\log}_{U})^{-1}.

2.2.
Weighted Lebesgue Space and Muckenhoupt Weights

For
1<p<\infty
and a weight
\omega\in L^{p}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
\omega^{-1}\in L^{p^{\prime}}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we define the weighted Lebesgue spaces

\displaystyle L^{p}_{\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{n}):=\{f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\colon\omega f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\}

equipped with the norm
\|f\|_{p,\omega}:=\|f\omega\|_{p}
. In particular, we treat the weight
\omega
as a multiplier. The dual space of
L^{p}_{\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is
L^{p^{\prime}}_{1/\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Both
L^{p}_{\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
L^{p^{\prime}}_{1/\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
are Banach spaces and continuously embedded into
L^{1}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Let
W^{1,p}_{\omega}(\Omega)
be the weighted Sobolev space which consists of functions
u\in W^{1,1}(\Omega)
such that
u,|\nabla u|\in L^{p}_{\omega}(\Omega)
, equipped with the norm
\|u\|_{W^{1,p}_{\omega}(\Omega)}=\|u\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}(\Omega)}
. Let
W^{1,p}_{0,\omega}(\Omega)
denote the subspace of
W^{1,p}_{\omega}(\Omega)
of functions with zero traces on
\partial\Omega
.

For
1<p<\infty
, a weight
\mu\in L^{1}_{{\mathrm{loc}}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is called an
\mathcal{A}_{p}
-Muckenhoupt weight if

\displaystyle[\mu]_{\mathcal{A}_{p}}:=\sup_{B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\mu\,dx\right)\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\mu^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\,dx\right)^{p-1}<\infty.

If
\mu
is an
\mathcal{A}_{p}
-Muckenhoupt weight, then the maximal operator is bounded on
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mu)
for
1<p<\infty
. We point out some properties for a Muckenhoupt weight related to its logarithmic means. If
\omega^{p}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{p}
-Muckenhoupt weight, then from Jensen’s inequality,

(2.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\omega^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}&\leq[\omega^{p}]^{\frac{1}{p}}_{\mathcal{A}_{p}}\langle{\omega}\rangle^{\log}_{B},\\
\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\omega^{-p^{\prime}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}&\leq[\omega^{p}]^{\frac{1}{p}}_{\mathcal{A}_{p}}\langle{\omega^{-1}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}=\dfrac{[\omega^{p}]^{\frac{1}{p}}_{\mathcal{A}_{p}}}{\langle{\omega}\rangle^{\log}_{B}}.\end{split}

Conversely, if (
2.1
) holds, then
\omega^{p}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{p}
-Muckenhoupt weight, since
\langle{\omega}\rangle^{\log}_{B}\langle{\omega^{-1}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}=1
holds.

2.3.
Parametrization of the Boundary

Let
\Omega
be
(\delta,4R)
–Lipschitz and
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
. Then there exists a Lipschitz map
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as in Definition
1.1
. By translation, without loss of generality we assume
x_{0}=0
and
\psi(0)=0
. We define
\Psi:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
as

(2.2)

\displaystyle\Psi(x^{\prime},x_{n})=(x^{\prime},x_{n}-\psi(x^{\prime}))\quad\text{for }(x^{\prime},x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

and so there hold
\Psi(\partial\Omega\cap B_{4R}(0))\subset\{(y^{\prime},y_{n}):y_{n}=0\}
,
\Psi(\overline{\Omega}\cap B_{4R}(0))\subset\{(y^{\prime},y_{n}):y_{n}\geq 0\}
and
\Psi(0)=0
. The mapping
\Psi
is invertible, with a Lipschitz continuous inverse
\Psi^{-1}
. We easily obtain

(2.3)

\displaystyle\nabla\Psi(x^{\prime},x_{n})=\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\
-\nabla\psi(x^{\prime})&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&\dots&0\\
0&\ddots&&\vdots\\
\vdots&&1&0\\
-\partial_{x_{1}}\psi(x^{\prime})&\dots&-\partial_{x_{n-1}}\psi(x^{\prime})&1\end{pmatrix}

for
(x^{\prime},x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where the right-hand side of (
2.3
) is an
n\times n
matrix. In particular,
\det(\nabla\Psi(x))=1
and so
|\Psi(B)|=|B|
for each ball
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Note that
|\mathrm{Id}-(\nabla\Psi)(x)|\leq n\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}
.

Now, we provide some geometric properties related to the maps
\psi
and
\Psi
which will be used throughout the paper.

Remark 2.1
.

From now on, we implicitly use the following properties. If we assume

(2.4)

\displaystyle\Omega\,\,\text{ is }\,\,(\delta,4R)\text{--Lipschitz with }\,\,\delta\in\left[0,\frac{1}{2n}\right]\,\,\text{ and }\,\,R>0,

Then for any induced map
\Psi
from the Lipschitz map
\psi
assigned to given
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
, we have
{\lvert{\mathrm{Id}-\nabla\psi}\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{2}
,

(2.5)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}B\subset\Psi(B)\subset 2B

for all ball
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and the following measure density properties also hold:

(2.6)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<r\leq 4R}\sup_{y\in\Omega}\dfrac{|B_{r}(y)|}{|\Omega\cap B_{r}(y)|}\leq 4^{n}

and

(2.7)

\displaystyle\inf_{0<r\leq 4R}\inf_{y\in\partial\Omega}\dfrac{|B_{r}(y)\cap\Omega^{c}|}{|B_{r}(y)|}\geq 4^{-n}.

2.4.
Preliminary Estimates

We first consider a weighted Poincaré inequality with partial zero boundary values. The corresponding mean value version is given in
[
BDGN21
]
.

Proposition 2.2

(Weighted Poincaré inequality at boundary)
.

Let
1<p<\infty
and
\theta\in(0,1)
be such that
\theta p\geq\max{\{{1,\frac{np}{n+p}}\}}
. Moreover, let
B_{r}=B_{r}(x_{0})
with
x_{0}\in\Omega
and
B_{\frac{3}{2}r}(x_{0})\not\subset\Omega
. Assume that
\Omega
is
(\delta,3r)
-Lipschitz with
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{2n}]
and that
\omega
is a weight on
B_{3r}
with

(2.8)

\displaystyle\sup_{B^{\prime}\subset B_{3r}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B^{\prime}}\omega^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B^{\prime}}\omega^{-(\theta p)^{\prime}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{(\theta p)^{\prime}}}\leq c_{1}.

Then for any
v\in W^{1,p}_{\omega}(B_{2r}\cap\Omega)
with
v=0
on
\partial\Omega\cap B_{2r}
,

(2.9)

\displaystyle\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}\cap\Omega}\left|\dfrac{v}{r}\right|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq c\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}\cap\Omega}(|\nabla v|\omega)^{\theta p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta p}}

holds with
c=c(n,p,c_{1})
.

Proof.

Since
v\in W^{1,p}_{\omega}(\Omega_{2r})
with
v=0
on
\partial\Omega\cap B_{2r}
, we can take the zero extension of
v
on the set
B_{2r}\setminus\Omega
. Since
B_{\frac{3r}{2}}(x_{0})\not\subset\Omega
, by (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) in Remark
2.1
, for
A:=B_{2r}\setminus\Omega
,
|A|\eqsim|B_{2r}\cap\Omega|
holds. Then
\langle{v}\rangle_{A}=0
holds, and so by Remark
2.1
, Proposition 3 in
[
BDGN21
]
and Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}\cap\Omega}\left|\dfrac{v}{r}\right|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\left|\dfrac{v-\langle{v}\rangle_{B_{2r}}}{r}\right|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\left|\dfrac{\langle{v}\rangle_{B_{2r}}-\langle{v}\rangle_{A}}{r}\right|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}(|\nabla v|\omega)^{\theta p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta p}}+\left[\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{A}\left|\dfrac{v(y)-\langle{v}\rangle_{B_{2r}}}{r}\right|\,dy\right)^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Here, using
|A|\eqsim|B_{2r}\cap\Omega|\eqsim|B_{2r}|
, Hölder’s inequality, (
2.8
) and Proposition 3 in
[
BDGN21
]
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{A}\left|\dfrac{v(y)-\langle{v}\rangle_{B_{2r}}}{r}\right|\,dy\right)^{p}\omega(x)^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\left[\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\left|\dfrac{v(y)-\langle{v}\rangle_{B_{2r}}}{r}\right|^{p}\omega(y)^{p}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\omega(y)^{-p^{\prime}}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\right]^{p}\omega(x)^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad\eqsim\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\left|\dfrac{v-\langle{v}\rangle_{B_{2r}}}{r}\right|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dy\right)\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\omega^{-p^{\prime}}\,dy\right)^{p-1}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}\omega^{p}\,dx\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{2r}}(|\nabla v|\omega)^{\theta p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}}.

Now, since
v=|\nabla v|=0
on
B_{2r}\setminus\Omega
and
|B_{2r}\cap\Omega|\eqsim|B_{2r}|
, we have (
2.9
).
∎

Let us collect a few auxiliary results from
[
BDGN21
]
that will be used later.
It follows from
[
BDGN21
, (3.24)]
with
\omega={\lvert{{\mathbb{M}}}\rvert}
that

(2.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Lambda^{-1}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{M}}}\rvert}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}&\leq{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{M}}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}}\rvert}\leq\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{M}}}\rvert}}\rangle^{\log}_{B},\\
\Lambda^{-2}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}&\leq{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}}\rvert}\leq\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}.\end{aligned}

Moreover, by monotonicity of the scalar versions of
\exp
and
\log
we have

(2.11)

\displaystyle\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}

\displaystyle\leq\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}.

Lemma 2.3
.

[
BDGN21
, Lemma 4]

For a matrix-valued weight
\mathbb{M}
and
\omega=|\mathbb{M}|
we have

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\lvert\log\omega(x)-\left<\log\omega\right>_{B}|\,dx\leq 2\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\lvert\log\mathbb{M}(x)-\left<\log\mathbb{M}\right>_{B}|\,dx

and so
\lvert\log\omega|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}\leq 2\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}
.

The next results provides a qualitative John-Nirenberg type inequality.

Lemma 2.4
.

[
BDGN21
, Proposition 5]

There exist constants
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{1}(n,\Lambda)>0
and
c_{2}=c_{2}(n,\Lambda)>0
such that the following holds: If
t\geq 1
and
\mathbb{M}
is a matrix-valued weight with
\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}}{t}
, then we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\left(\dfrac{|\mathbb{M}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{M}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}|}{|\langle{\mathbb{M}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}|}\right)^{t}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{t}}\leq c_{2}t\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}.

The same holds with
\omega
instead of
\mathbb{M}
.

The following results is a minor modification of
[
BDGN21
, Proposition 6]
.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
\kappa_{1}
and
c_{2}
be as in Lemma
2.4
. Then with a constant
\beta=\beta(n,\Lambda)=\min\left\{\kappa_{1},1/c_{2}\right\}>0
, the following holds for all weights
\omega
.

(a)

If
\lvert\log\omega|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}\leq\frac{\beta}{\gamma}
with
\gamma\geq 1
, then there holds

\displaystyle\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\omega^{\gamma}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\leq 2\langle{\omega}\rangle^{\log}_{B}\quad\text{and}\quad\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\omega^{-\gamma}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\leq 2\dfrac{1}{\langle{\omega}\rangle^{\log}_{B}}.

(b)

If
\lvert\log\omega|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}\leq\beta\min\{\frac{1}{p},\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}\}
with
1<p<\infty
, then
\omega^{p}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{p}
–Muckenhoupt weight and

\displaystyle[\omega^{p}]^{\frac{1}{p}}_{\mathcal{A}_{p}}=\sup_{B^{\prime}\subset B}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B^{\prime}}\omega^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B^{\prime}}\omega^{-p^{\prime}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\leq 4.

(c)

Let
1<p<\infty
and
\theta\in(0,1)
be such that
\theta p>1
. If
\lvert\log\omega|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}\leq\beta\min\{\frac{1}{p},1-\frac{1}{\theta p}\}
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{B^{\prime}\subset B}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B^{\prime}}\omega^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B^{\prime}}\omega^{-(\theta p)^{\prime}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{(\theta p)^{\prime}}}\leq 4.

Proof.

The proof is the same as in
[
BDGN21
, Proposition 6]
with minimal changes due to the localized versions.
∎

Remark 2.6
.

Using the relation
\log(\mathbb{M}^{-1})=-\log(\mathbb{M})
and
\log(\omega^{-1})=-\log(\omega)
, we can apply Lemma
2.4
and Lemma
2.5
also to
\mathbb{M}^{-1}
and
\omega^{-1}
.

2.5.
Summary on Orlicz Functions

We say that a function
\tilde{\varphi}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is an N-function if
\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is a right-continuous and non-decreasing function with
\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}(t)=\infty
such that
\tilde{\varphi}(t)=\int^{t}_{0}\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}(\tau)\,d\tau
. An N-function is called to satisfy the
\Delta_{2}
-condition if there is a constant
c>1
such that
\tilde{\varphi}(2t)\leq c\tilde{\varphi}(t)
.

We now define a specific N-function

\displaystyle\varphi(t):=\dfrac{1}{p}t^{p}.

Then we denote

\displaystyle A(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\dfrac{\varphi^{\prime}(|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\xi=|\xi|^{p-2}\xi,

\displaystyle V(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\dfrac{\varphi^{\prime}(|\xi|)}{|\xi|}}\xi=|\xi|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\xi.

Let
\tilde{\varphi}^{*}
be the conjugate of an N-function
\tilde{\varphi}
as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}^{*}(t):=\sup_{s\geq 0}(ts-\tilde{\varphi}(s)),\quad t\geq 0,

and so
\varphi^{*}(t)=\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}t^{p^{\prime}}
.

We also need the shifted N-functions as introduced in
[
DE08
,
DK08
,
DFTW20
,
BDGN21
]
. For
a\geq 0
we define
\varphi_{a}
as

(2.12)

\displaystyle\varphi_{a}(t):=\int^{t}_{0}\dfrac{\varphi^{\prime}(a\vee s)}{a\vee s}s\,ds.

Here
s_{1}\vee s_{2}:=\max\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
for
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. We call
a
the
shift
. So for
t\leq a
, then function
\varphi_{a}(t)
is quadratic in
t
.
One can see that
\varphi_{0}=\varphi
holds, and
a\eqsim b
implies
\varphi_{a}(t)\eqsim\varphi_{b}(t)
. Also, we have

(2.13)

\displaystyle\varphi_{a}(t)

\displaystyle\eqsim(a\vee t)^{p-2}t^{2},

(2.14)

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(t)

\displaystyle\eqsim(a\vee t)^{p-2}t,

(2.15)

\displaystyle(\varphi_{a})^{*}

\displaystyle=(\varphi^{*})_{\varphi^{\prime}(a)},

(2.16)

\displaystyle(\varphi_{|\xi|})^{*}

\displaystyle=(\varphi^{*})_{\varphi^{\prime}(|\xi|)}

with constants depending only on
p
. Moreover, for
a\geq 0
, the collection of
\varphi_{a}
and
(\varphi_{a})^{*}
satisfy the
\Delta_{2}
-condition with a
\Delta_{2}
-constant independent of
a
.

We also have Young’s inequality. For every
\varepsilon>0
there exists
c(\varepsilon)=c(\varepsilon,p)\geq 1
such that for all
s,t,a\geq 0

(2.17)

\displaystyle st\leq c(\varepsilon)(\varphi_{a})^{*}(s)+\varepsilon\varphi_{a}(t).

Here,
c(\varepsilon)\eqsim\max{\{{\varepsilon^{-1},\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}}\}}
. Similarly, considering the relations
\varphi_{a}(t)\eqsim\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(t)t
and
(\varphi_{a})^{*}\eqsim(t\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(t))
, we have

(2.18)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s)t\leq c(\varepsilon)\varphi_{a}(s)+\varepsilon\varphi_{a}(t),\\
\varphi^{\prime}_{a}(s)t\leq\varepsilon\varphi_{a}(s)+\tilde{c}(\varepsilon)\varphi_{a}(t)\end{split}

for all
s,t,a\geq 0
, where
\tilde{c}(\varepsilon)\eqsim\max{\{{\varepsilon^{-1},\varepsilon^{1-p}}\}}
.
Moreover, the following relation holds for
a\geq 0
:

(2.19)

\displaystyle\varphi_{a}(\lambda a)\eqsim\begin{cases}\lambda^{2}\varphi(a),\quad&\text{for }\lambda<1,\\
\varphi(\lambda a)\quad&\text{for }\lambda\geq 1.\end{cases}

We emphasize the relation between
A,V
and
\varphi_{a}
as in the following:

Lemma 2.7

(
[
DFTW20
, Lemma 41]
)
.

For all
P,Q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we have

\displaystyle(A(P)-A(Q))\cdot(P-Q)\eqsim|V(P)-V(Q)|^{2}\eqsim\varphi_{|Q|}(|P-Q|)\eqsim(\varphi^{*})_{|A(Q)|}(|A(P)-A(Q)|),

\displaystyle A(Q)\cdot Q=|V(Q)|^{2}\eqsim\varphi_{|Q|}(|Q|)\eqsim\varphi(|Q|)

and

\displaystyle|A(P)-A(Q)|\eqsim(\varphi_{|Q|})^{\prime}(|P-Q|)\eqsim\varphi^{\prime}_{|P|\vee|Q|}(|P-Q|),

where the implicit constants depend only on
p
.

We usually use the following
change of shift
:

Lemma 2.8

(Change of shift,
[
DFTW20
, Corollary 44]
)
.

For
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
c_{\varepsilon}=c_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon,p)
such that for all
P,Q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{|P|}(t)

\displaystyle\leq c_{\varepsilon}\varphi_{|Q|}(t)+\varepsilon|V(P)-V(Q)|^{2},

\displaystyle\left(\varphi_{|P|}\right)^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\leq c_{\varepsilon}\left(\varphi_{|Q|}\right)^{*}(t)+\varepsilon|V(P)-V(Q)|^{2},

where
c_{\varepsilon}=c(\varepsilon,p)
.

Also, we need the following
removal of shift
.

Lemma 2.9

(Removal of shift,
[
BDGN21
, Lemma 13]
)
.

For all
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
t\geq 0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, we have

(2.20)

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}_{|a|}(t)\leq\varphi^{\prime}\left(\dfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)\vee(\varepsilon\varphi^{\prime}(|a|)),

(2.21)

\displaystyle\varphi_{|a|}(t)\leq\varepsilon\varphi(|a|)+c\varepsilon\varphi\left(\dfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\right),

(2.22)

\displaystyle(\varphi_{|a|})^{*}(t)\leq\varepsilon\varphi(|a|)+c\varepsilon\varphi^{*}\left(\dfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\right)

with
c=c(p)
.

3.
Global Maximal Regularity Estimates

We now provide global maximal regularity estimates for the weak solutions of our weighted
p
-Laplace equation for the linear case
p=2
as well as the non-linear case
p\in(1,\infty)
. Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be
(\delta,R)
–Lipschitz and
{\mathbb{M}}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}
be a degenerate elliptic matrix-valued weight with uniformly bounded condition number (
1.3
). Recall, that
\omega(x):={\lvert{{\mathbb{M}}(x)}\rvert}
. Note, that (
1.3
) is equivalent to

(3.1)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-1}\omega(x)|\xi|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{M}(x)\xi|\leq\omega(x)|\xi|

\displaystyle\text{for all }\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

and also

(3.2)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-1}\omega(x)\text{Id}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{M}(x)\leq\omega(x)\text{Id}

for all
x\in\Omega
.

If we assume that
\log\mathbb{M}
has a small
{\mathrm{BMO}}
-norm, i.e., assume

(3.3)

\displaystyle\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(\Omega)}\leq\kappa,

we also have
\lvert\log\omega|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(\Omega)}\leq 2\kappa
by Lemma
2.3
.
Suppose that
\kappa
is so small such that by Lemma
2.5
,
\omega^{p}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{p}
-Muckenhoupt weight. Then
C^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega)
is dense in
W^{1,p}_{0,\omega}(\Omega)
. Now, let
u\in W^{1,p}_{0,\omega}(\Omega)
be the weak solution of (
1.2
) with
F\in L^{p}_{\omega}(\Omega)
, i.e., if we denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(x,\xi):=|\mathbb{M}(x)\xi|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}^{2}(x)\xi=\mathbb{M}(x)A(\mathbb{M}(x)\xi),

then

(3.4)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{A}(x,\nabla u)\cdot\nabla\xi\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{A}(x,F)\cdot\nabla\xi\,dx

for all
\xi\in W^{1,p}_{0,\omega}(\Omega)
. Since
\omega^{p}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{p}
-Muckenhoupt weight, the existence and uniqueness of
u
is guaranteed by standard arguments from the calculus of variations.

3.1.
Caccioppoli and Reverse Hölder’s Inequality

We start with the standard Caccioppoli estimates associated with our degenerate
p
-Laplacian problem. We fix a ball
B_{0}:=B_{R}(x_{0})
with
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
. Then since
\Omega
is
(\delta,4R)
–Lipschitz, there exists a coordinate system
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}
such that
x_{0}=0
in this coordinate system, and with the assigned Lipschitz map
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
we have (
1.14
) with
4R
instead of
R
. Let
u\in W^{1,p}_{\omega}(4B_{0}\cap\Omega)
be a weak solution of

(3.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} -\operatorname{div}\mathcal{A}(x,\nabla u)&=-\operatorname{div}\mathcal{A}(x,F)&\qquad&\text{in }4B_{0}\cap\Omega,\\
u&=0&&\text{on }\partial\Omega\cap(4B_{0}).\end{aligned}

From now on, let
B_{r}=B_{r}(\tilde{x})
denote an arbitrary ball with
\tilde{x}\in\overline{\Omega}
and
4B_{r}\subset 2B_{0}
. Denoting
a\Omega_{r}=aB_{r}\cap 2B_{0}\cap\Omega
for
a\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we have the following:

Proposition 3.1

(Caccioppoli inequality)
.

Let
u\in W^{1,p}_{\omega}(4B_{0}\cap\Omega)
be a weak solution of (
3.5
) and
B_{r}=B_{r}(\tilde{x})
denote an arbitrary ball with
\tilde{x}\in\overline{\Omega}
and
4B_{r}\subset 2B_{0}
.

(1)

(Interior case) If
2B_{r}\subset\Omega
, then we have

(3.6)

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}|\nabla u|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\leq c\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}\left|\dfrac{u-\langle{u}\rangle_{2\Omega_{r}}}{r}\right|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx+c\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}|F|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx.

(2)

(Boundary case) Assume (
2.4
). If
2B_{r}\not\subset\Omega
, then we have

(3.7)

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}|\nabla u|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\leq c\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}\left|\dfrac{u}{r}\right|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx+c\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}|F|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx.

In both cases
c=c(n,p,\Lambda)
.

Proof.

First, (
3.6
) follows from
[
BDGN21
, Proposition 8]
. To show (
3.7
), let
\eta
be a smooth cut-off function with
\chi_{B_{r}}\leq\eta\leq\chi_{2B_{r}}
and
|\nabla\eta|\leq\frac{c}{r}
. Testing
\eta^{p}u\in W^{1,p}_{0,\omega}(2\Omega_{r})
in (
3.5
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{2\Omega_{r}}|\mathbb{M}\nabla u|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}\nabla u\cdot\mathbb{M}\nabla(\eta^{p}u)\,dx=\int_{2\Omega_{r}}|\mathbb{M}F|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}F\cdot\mathbb{M}\nabla(\eta^{p}u)\,dx.

Using (
3.1
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{2\Omega_{r}}\eta^{p}|\nabla u|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{2\Omega_{r}}\eta^{p-1}|\nabla u|^{p-1}\left|\dfrac{u}{r}\right|\omega^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{2\Omega_{r}}\eta^{p}|F|^{p-1}|\nabla u|\omega^{p}\,dx+\int_{2\Omega_{r}}\eta^{p-1}|F|^{p-1}\left|\dfrac{u}{r}\right|\omega^{p}\,dx.

By Young’s inequality, absorb the term
\eta^{p}|\nabla u|^{p}\omega^{p}
into left-hand side, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{r}}|\nabla u|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\lesssim\int_{2\Omega_{r}}\left|\dfrac{u}{r}\right|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx+\int_{2\Omega_{r}}|F|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx.

Now, by (
2.6
), we have
|\Omega_{r}|\eqsim|B_{r}|\eqsim|2B_{r}|\eqsim|2\Omega_{r}|
. Then (
3.7
) follows.
∎

Now, we can provide the reverse Hölder’s inequality.

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume (
2.4
). There exists
\kappa_{2}=\kappa_{2}(n,p,\Lambda)>0
and
\theta\in(0,1)
such that if
\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(3B_{r})}\leq\kappa_{2}
, then

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}|\nabla u|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx\leq c\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}(|\nabla u|\omega)^{\theta p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}}+c\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}|F|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx

holds with
c=c(n,p,\Lambda)
.

Proof.

If
\frac{3}{2}B_{r}\subset\Omega
, then we can select small
\kappa_{2}
such that Lemma
2.5
(c) holds true to use Proposition 3 in
[
BDGN21
]
. This and Proposition
3.1
proves the claim.

If
\frac{3}{2}B_{r}\not\subset\Omega
, using Proposition
2.2
instead of Proposition 3 in
[
BDGN21
]
, we again prove the claim.
∎

Now, we have the following higher integrability.

Corollary 3.3

(Higher integrability)
.

Assume (
2.4
). There exist
\kappa_{2}=\kappa_{2}(n,p,\Lambda)>0
and
s=s(n,p,\Lambda)\in(1,2)
such that if
\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(3B_{r})}\leq\kappa_{2}
, then

\displaystyle\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}(|\nabla u|^{p}\omega^{p})^{s}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}\leq c\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}|\nabla u|^{p}\omega^{p}\,dx+c\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}(|F|^{p}\omega^{p})^{s}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}

holds with
c=c(n,p,\Lambda)
.

Proof.

After extending
\nabla u=F=0
in
2B_{r}\setminus\Omega
, and considering
|\Omega_{r}|\eqsim|B_{r}|
and
|2\Omega_{r}|\eqsim|2B_{r}|
, Gehring’s lemma (e.g.
[
Giu03
, Theorem 6.6]
) implies the conclusion.
∎

3.2.
Comparison Estimate at Boundary

In this section, we only prove boundary comparison estimates, since the interior estimates are proved in
[
BDGN21
]
.
Let us assume
\mathbb{M}F\in L^{q}(\Omega)
. Choose a cut-off function
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{0})
with

(3.8)

\displaystyle\chi_{\frac{1}{2}B_{0}}\leq\eta\leq\chi_{B_{0}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\nabla\eta\|_{\infty}\leq c/R.

Define
z
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
as follows: first let
z
on
B_{0}\cap\Omega
be such that

(3.9)

\displaystyle z:=u\eta^{p^{\prime}}

and take the zero extension for
z
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus(B_{0}\cap\Omega)
, if necessary. Also, we denote

(3.10)

\displaystyle g:=\eta^{p^{\prime}}\nabla u-\nabla z=-u\nabla(\eta^{p^{\prime}})=-up^{\prime}\eta^{p^{\prime}-1}\nabla\eta.

Then we have the following estimate:

(3.11)

\displaystyle|g|\lesssim\dfrac{|u|}{R}.

For the convenience of notation, we write

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\mathbb{M}}\rangle^{\log}_{B_{r}},

\displaystyle\omega_{B_{r}}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\omega}\rangle^{\log}_{B_{r}},

and so

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=|\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}\xi|^{p-2}\mathbb{M}^{2}_{B_{r}}\xi=\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}A(\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}\xi),

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle:=V(\mathbb{M}(x)\xi),

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{B_{r}}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=V(\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}\xi).

Then we have the following relations for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
:

(3.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(x,\xi)\cdot\xi

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{V}(x,\xi)|^{2},

(3.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\xi)\cdot\xi

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{V}_{B_{r}}(\xi)|^{2},

(3.14)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\xi)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\omega^{p}_{B_{r}}|\xi|^{p-1}

and by
[
BDGN21
, Section 3]
,

(3.15)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-1}\omega_{B_{r}}|\xi|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}\xi|\leq\omega_{B_{r}}|\xi|,

(3.16)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-1}\omega_{B_{r}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}|\leq\omega_{B_{r}}.

Summing up the above result, we have
[
BDGN21
, Lemma 16]
as follows:
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and all
x\in B_{r}
there holds

(3.17)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\xi)-\mathcal{A}(x,\xi)|\lesssim\dfrac{|\mathbb{M}-\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}(x)|}{|\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}|}\left(|\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\xi)|+|\mathcal{A}(x,\xi)|\right).

Before introducing the reference problem, we provide the following lemma for the well-posedness:

Lemma 3.4
.

Assuming (
2.4
), there exists
\kappa_{3}=\kappa_{3}(n,p,\Lambda)>0
and
s=s(n,p,\Lambda)\in(1,2)
such that if
\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(4B_{r})}\leq\kappa_{3}
, then
with
2\Omega_{r}=2B_{r}\cap B_{0}\cap\Omega
there holds

(3.18)

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}(|\nabla u|\omega_{B_{r}})^{p}\,dx\lesssim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}(|\nabla u|\omega)^{p}\,dx+\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}(|F|\omega)^{ps}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}.

Proof.

Using Hölder’s inequality,
|\Omega_{r}|\eqsim|B_{r}|
and Lemma
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}(|\nabla u|\omega_{B_{r}})^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}(|\nabla u|\omega)^{ps}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}(\omega_{B_{r}}\omega^{-1})^{ps^{\prime}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}(|\nabla u|\omega)^{ps}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{r}}(\omega_{B_{r}}\omega^{-1})^{ps^{\prime}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}(|\nabla u|\omega)^{ps}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}.

Then Corollary
3.3
yields the conclusion.
∎

Now, let
h\in W^{1,p}_{\omega_{B_{r}}}(\Omega_{r})
be the weak solution of

(3.19)

\displaystyle\begin{split}-\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\nabla h)\right)&=0\quad\text{in }\Omega_{r},\\
h&=z\quad\text{on }\partial\Omega_{r}.\end{split}

Then
h
is the unique minimizer of

(3.20)

\displaystyle w\mapsto\int_{\Omega_{r}}\varphi(|\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}\nabla w|)\,dx

with boundary data
w=z
on
\partial\Omega_{r}
. Now, we provide the first comparison estimate. Recall that
B_{r}=B_{r}(\tilde{x})
,
B_{0}=B_{R}(x_{0})
,
4B_{r}\subset 2B_{0}
,
\tilde{x}\in\overline{\Omega}
and
z,h
are given by (
3.9
) and (
3.19
), respectively. Moreover, as
[
BDGN21
, Eq. (3.25)]
, we have

(3.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}-&\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\nabla z)-\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\nabla h)\right)\\
&\quad=-\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\nabla z)-\mathcal{A}(\cdot,\nabla z)\right)-\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{A}(\cdot,\nabla z)-\mathcal{A}(\cdot,\nabla z+g)\right)\\
&\quad\quad-\eta^{p}\operatorname{div}\left(\mathcal{A}(\cdot,F)\right)-\nabla(\eta^{p})\cdot\mathcal{A}(\cdot,\nabla u)\end{split}

on
\Omega_{r}
, in the distributional sense.

Proposition 3.5

(First comparison at boundary)
.

Assuming (
2.4
), let
h
be as in (
3.19
) and
z
be as in (
3.9
). There exist
s>1
and
\kappa_{4}=\kappa_{4}(n,p,\Lambda)\in(0,1)
, such that if
\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(4B_{r})}\leq\kappa_{4}
holds, then for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
we have

(3.22)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}&|\mathcal{V}_{B_{r}}(\nabla h)-\mathcal{V}_{B_{r}}(\nabla z)|^{2}\,dx\leq c\left(\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|^{2}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B_{r})}+\varepsilon\right)\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}(|\nabla z|^{p}\omega^{p})^{s}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad+c\,C^{*}(\varepsilon)\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}\left(\dfrac{|u|^{p}}{R^{p}}\omega^{p}\right)^{s}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}+c\,C^{*}(\varepsilon)\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2\Omega_{r}}(|F|^{p}\omega^{p})^{s}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}\end{split}

for some
c=c(n,p,\Lambda)
, and
C^{*}(\varepsilon)=\max\left\{\varepsilon^{1-p},\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\right\}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of
[
BDGN21
, Proposition 17]
. Observe that
\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(4B_{r})}\leq\kappa_{4}
implies that (
3.19
) is well-defined. Testing
z-h
to (
3.19
) and (
3.5
), by (
3.21
) it follows that

(3.23)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{0}&:=\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\nabla z)-\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\nabla h)\right)\cdot(\nabla z-\nabla h)\,dx\\
&=\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\nabla z)-\mathcal{A}(x,\nabla z)\right)\cdot(\nabla z-\nabla h)\,dx\\
&\quad+\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}\left(\mathcal{A}(x,\nabla z)-\mathcal{A}(x,\nabla z+g)\right)\cdot(\nabla z-\nabla h)\,dx\\
&\quad+\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}\mathcal{A}(x,F)\cdot(\nabla(\eta^{p}z)-\nabla(\eta^{p}h))\,dx\\
&\quad+\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}\nabla(\eta^{p})\cdot\mathcal{A}(x,\nabla u)(z-h)\,dx=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.\end{split}

By Lemma
2.7
, we have

(3.24)

\displaystyle I_{0}\eqsim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}|\mathcal{V}_{B_{r}}(\nabla h)-\mathcal{V}_{B_{r}}(\nabla z)|^{2}\,dx\eqsim\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}\varphi_{|\nabla z|}(|\nabla z-\nabla h|)\omega^{p}_{B_{r}}\,dx.

To estimate
I_{1}
, arguing as in the proof of
[
BDGN21
, Proposition 17]
, we have

(3.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{1}&=\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{B_{r}}(\nabla z)-\mathcal{A}(x,\nabla z)\right)\cdot(\nabla z-\nabla h)\,dx\\
&\leq\sigma\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}\varphi_{|\nabla z|}(|\nabla z-\nabla h|)\omega^{p}_{B_{r}}\,dx\\
&\quad+c(\sigma)\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{\Omega_{r}}\left(\dfrac{|\mathbb{M}-\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}|}{|\mathbb{M}_{B_{r}}|}\right)^{2}\varphi(|\nabla z|)\left(\dfrac{\omega^{p}_{B_{r}}}{\omega^{p}}+\dfrac{\omega^{p}}{\omega^{p}_{B_{r}}}+\dfrac{\omega^{p^{\prime}}}{\omega^{p^{\prime}}_{B_{r}}}\right)\omega^{p}\,dx=I_{1,1}+I_{1,2}\end{split}

for any
\sigma\in(0,1)
. Now,
I_{1,1}
is absorbed to the left-hand side
I_{0}
by choosing
\sigma=\sigma(n,p,\Lambda)
sufficiently small, and so
c(\sigma)=c
. For
I_{1,2}
, we first assume
\lvert\log\mathbb{M}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B_{r})}\leq\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{1}(n,p,\Lambda)
and then use Lemma
2.4
, together with
|\Omega_{r}|\eqsim|B_{r}|
(the measure density of
\Omega_{r}
to
B_{r}
) from (
2.4
) and (
2.6
), to have

(3.26)
