\mathbb{A}\nabla(\eta^{2}u)=\mathbb{A}\nabla u
on
2B_{0}\cap\Omega
and
|\mathbb{A}\nabla u|\eqsim\omega^{2}|\nabla u|
hold, we conclude

(3.140)

\displaystyle\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B_{0}\cap\Omega}(\omega|\nabla u|)^{q}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq c\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2B_{0}\cap\Omega}(\omega|\nabla u|)^{\theta q}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta q}}+c\left(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{2B_{0}\cap\Omega}(\omega|F|)^{q}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{q}},

which is analogous to (
3.110
). Now, by applying the argument of the proof of Proposition
3.14
, we can change the exponent
\theta q
to 1. Then similar to the proof of Theorem
1.4
, using the covering argument, together with the assumptions

(3.141)

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{A}\,\,\,\text{is}\,\,\,\left(\delta\min\left\{\frac{1}{q},1-\frac{1}{q}\right\},R\right)\text{--vanishing and}

(3.142)

\displaystyle\Omega\,\,\text{is}\,\,\left(\delta\min\left\{\frac{1}{q},1-\frac{1}{q}\right\},R\right)\text{--Lipschitz},

we get (
1.18
). This proves Theorem
1.3
.
∎

4.
Sharpness and Smallness Condition

In this section we discuss the sharpness of our smallness condition and compare it to other type of conditions as found in
[
CMP18
,
CMP19
]
.

4.1.
Sharpness of the Linear Dependence

We have shown in Theorem
1.3
and Theorem
1.4
that reciprocal of the exponent
q
of higher integrability is linearly connected to the smallness condition on
\log{\mathbb{A}}
and
\partial\Omega
. In this section we show that this linear dependence is the best possible. It has been shown already in
[
BDGN21
, Section 4]
that the smallness on
\log{\mathbb{A}}
is necessary by means of analyzing the counterexample introduced by Meyers
[
Mey63
]
. Therefore, we concentrate in this article on the sharpness of the condition on
\Omega
. Since the effect already occurs in the unweighted case, we assume that
\mathbb{M}=\mathrm{Id}
. We provide a two dimensional example, but the principle generalizes to higher dimensions as well.

Example 4.1
.

For
n=2
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we consider the following type of the domain:

\displaystyle\Omega=\{x=(x_{1},x_{2})\in B_{1}(0):x_{2}>-\varepsilon|x_{1}|\}.

Then
\Omega
is
(\varepsilon,1)
-Lipschitz. Moreover, the assigned Lipschitz map for the origin is
\psi:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
\psi(x_{1})=-\varepsilon|x_{1}|
and so
\|\nabla\psi\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon
.

Now, for
\alpha:=\frac{\pi/2}{\pi/2+\tan^{-1}\varepsilon}
we define in polar coordinates
x=r(\cos\varphi,\sin\varphi)

\displaystyle u(x)=u(r,\varphi)=\cos\big(\alpha(\varphi-\tfrac{\pi}{2})\big)r^{\alpha}.

Then
u
is a solution of the equation

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta u&=0\quad\text{in }\Omega,\\
u&=0\quad\text{on }\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:x_{2}=-\varepsilon|x_{1}|\}\cap B_{1}(0).\end{split}

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta u&=\dfrac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial r^{2}}+\frac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial u}{\partial r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\dfrac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial\varphi^{2}}\\
&=\Big(\alpha(\alpha-1)r^{\alpha-2}+\frac{\alpha}{r}r^{\alpha-1}-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)\cos\big(\alpha(\varphi-\tfrac{\pi}{2})\big)r^{\alpha}=0.\end{split}

Moreover, with
e_{r}:=(\cos\varphi,\sin\varphi)
and
e_{\varphi}:=(-\sin\varphi,\cos\varphi)
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\nabla u|&={\bigg\lvert{\frac{\partial u}{\partial r}e_{r}+\frac{\partial u}{r\partial\varphi}e_{\varphi}}\bigg\rvert}\\
&={\big\lvert{\alpha\cos\big(\alpha(\varphi-\tfrac{\pi}{2})\big)r^{\alpha-1}e_{r}-\alpha\sin\big(\alpha(\varphi-\tfrac{\pi}{2})\big)r^{\alpha-1}e_{\varphi}}\big\rvert}=\alpha r^{\alpha-1}.\end{split}

Assume that
q>2
. Then
\nabla u\in L^{q,\infty}(B_{1}(0))
(Lorentz space or weak Lebesgue space) is equivalent to
(\alpha-1)q\geq-2
. This simplifies to

\displaystyle\nabla u\in L^{q,\infty}(B_{1}(0))\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\tan^{-1}\varepsilon\leq\frac{\pi}{q-2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\nabla u\in L^{q}(B_{1}(0))\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\tan^{-1}\varepsilon<\frac{\pi}{q-2}.

Note that for small
\varepsilon
, we have
\tan^{-1}\varepsilon\approx\varepsilon
.
This implies that the smallness assumptions (
1.17b
) and (
1.19
) are optimal.

4.2.
Comparison to the Condition of Cao, Mengesha and Phan

Let us assume that
{\mathbb{A}}\,:\,\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}}
with bounded condition number with
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}\leq\Lambda^{2}
. In this section we compare our smallness condition on the weight in terms of
\log{\mathbb{M}}
with the smallness condition on
{\mathbb{M}}
from Cao, Mengesha and Phan in
[
CMP18
,
CMP19
]
. In
[
CMP18
]
, they introduced the quantity
1
1

1

Cao, Mengesha and Phan do not use
\mu(x):={\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}
, but treat
\mu
as an independent function that satisfies the equivalence
\mu(x)\eqsim{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}
. However, choosing
\mu(x):={\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}
is always an equivalent valid choice.

(4.1)

\displaystyle{\lvert{\mathbb{A}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}}:=\sup_{B}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{A}}\rangle_{B}|^{2}\mu^{-1}(x)\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

in order to measure the oscillations of
{\mathbb{A}}
, where
\mu(x):={\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}
and the supremum is taken over all balls.
In addition to the smallness conditions Cao, Mengesha and Phan assume that
\mu:={\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
.

In
[
CMP19
]
they use the simpler quantity

(4.2)

\displaystyle{\lvert{\mathbb{A}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}_{\mu}}:=\sup_{B}\dfrac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{A}}\rangle_{B}|\,dx.

Note that by Hölder’s inequality

\displaystyle{\lvert{\mathbb{A}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq{\lvert{\mathbb{A}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}}.

In contrast our measure of oscillations is

(4.3)

\displaystyle{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}}=\sup_{B}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\,dx.

Due to
1\leq{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}\leq\Lambda^{2}
we have

(4.4)

\displaystyle\langle{\mu}\rangle_{B}\langle{\mu^{-1}}\rangle_{B}\leq\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\leq\Lambda^{2}\langle{\mu}\rangle_{B}\langle{\mu^{-1}}\rangle_{B}.

The following lemma shows that our smallness condition on
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}}
is weaker than the smallness condition on
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}}
combined with the
\mathcal{A}_{2}
-condition.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
{\mathbb{A}}\,:\,\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}}
be a weight with
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}\leq\Lambda^{2}
and
\mu:={\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
. Then for all balls
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
there holds

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq 4\,\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{A}}\rangle_{B}|^{2}\mu^{-1}(x)\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Moreover,
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}}\leq 4\,\Lambda^{2}[\mu]_{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}}
.

Proof.

We begin with

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf_{{\mathbb{A}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}}}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}-\log{\mathbb{A}}_{0}}\rvert}\,dx\leq 2\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}-\log(\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B})}\rvert}\,dx.

It has been shown e.g. in
[
Gha21
, Example 1]
that for all
{\mathbb{G}},{\mathbb{H}}\in{\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}}
there holds

\displaystyle{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{G}}-\log{\mathbb{H}}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\max{\{{{\lvert{{\mathbb{G}}^{-1}}\rvert},{\lvert{{\mathbb{H}}^{-1}}\rvert}}\}}{\lvert{{\mathbb{G}}-{\mathbb{H}}}\rvert}.

This implies

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\max{\{{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert},{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}^{-1}}\rvert}}\}}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\,dx+2{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}^{-1}}\rvert}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\,dx=:\textrm{I}+\textrm{II}.

By Hölder’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\textrm{I}=\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\bigg(\frac{1}{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}}\int_{B}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

On the other hand by Hölder’s inequality

\displaystyle\textrm{I}\leq{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}^{-1}}\rvert}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}^{-1}}\rvert}{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}\bigg(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}-\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}}\rvert}^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Due to
[
Bha07
, Exercise 1.5.10]
the mapping
{\mathbb{A}}\mapsto{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}
is convex on
{\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}}
. Thus, by Jensen’s inequality
0<\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}^{-1}\leq\langle{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rangle_{B}
and as a consequence
{\big\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle_{B}^{-1}}\big\rvert}\leq{\big\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rangle_{B}}\big\rvert}\leq\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}
.
Using this fact, we obtain for
II
the same estimate as for
I
. Combining all estimates proves the first claim. Taking the supremum over all balls
B
and using (
4.4
) proves the second claim.
∎

On the other hand we will show now that if
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}}
is small enough, then it controls
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}}
in a linear way.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
{\mathbb{A}}\,:\,\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}}
be a weight such
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}\leq\Lambda^{2}
. Then there exists
\delta=\delta(n,\Lambda)>0
such that the following holds: If
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}}\leq\delta
, then for all balls
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{{\lvert{B}\rvert}}{\mu(B)}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{A}}\rangle_{B}|^{2}\mu^{-1}(x)\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq c(n,\Lambda){\lvert{\log\mathbb{A}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}.

In particular,
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}(B)}\leq c(n,\Lambda){\lvert{\log\mathbb{A}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}
.

Proof.

Let
{\lvert{\log\mathbb{A}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}\leq\delta
. We choose
\delta>0
so small such that we can apply Lemma
2.4
(for
t=4
) and Lemma
2.5
(for
\gamma=2
).
By Lemma
2.5

(b)
we obtain
\mu={\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
with
[\mu]_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}=[{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}]_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\leq 16
. Thus, with (
4.4
) we have
\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\leq 16\,\Lambda^{2}
.
Recall that
\mu(B)=\int_{B}\mu\,dx=\int_{B}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}\,dx={\lvert{B}\rvert}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}
.
For all
{\mathbb{A}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}}
we estimate with Jensen’s inequality in the third step

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{A}}\rangle_{B}|^{2}\mu^{-1}(x)\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\bigg(\frac{1}{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{A}}\rangle_{B}|^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-{\mathbb{A}}_{0}|^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\bigg(\frac{1}{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}|\langle{{\mathbb{A}}-{\mathbb{A}}_{0}}\rangle_{B}|^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}=:\textrm{I}+\textrm{II}.

With Jensen’s inequality, Hölder’s inequality and
\mu^{-1}={\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}^{-1}\leq{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}
we obtain

II

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-{\mathbb{A}}_{0}|\,dx\,\bigg(\frac{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}^{-1}}\rangle_{B}}{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-{\mathbb{A}}_{0}|^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}^{-1}}\rangle_{B}}{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\big(\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\bigg(\frac{1}{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-{\mathbb{A}}_{0}|^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\big(\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\textrm{I}\leq\,4\,\Lambda\,\textrm{I}.

Overall, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{A}}\rangle_{B}|^{2}\mu^{-1}(x)\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+4\,\Lambda)\underbrace{\inf_{{\mathbb{A}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{>0}}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}|\mathbb{A}(x)-{\mathbb{A}}_{0}|^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{=:\textrm{III}}.

Now, choosing
{\mathbb{A}}_{0}=\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}
and using Hölder’s inequality we obtain

III

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{\langle{{\lvert{\mathbb{A}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}}\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{A}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}|^{2}{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}(x)}\rvert}^{-1}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}}\rvert}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}^{-2}}\rangle_{B}^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\langle{{\lvert{\mathbb{A}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg(\mathop{}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.83331pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.1215pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.82639pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-3.0mu\int_{B}\frac{|\mathbb{A}(x)-\langle{\mathbb{A}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}|^{4}}{{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}}\rvert}^{4}}\,dx\bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

With Lemma
2.5

(a)
(with
-\gamma=-2
), (
2.10
) and (
2.11
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}}\rvert}\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}^{-2}}\rangle_{B}^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\langle{{\lvert{\mathbb{A}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\,{\lvert{\langle{{\mathbb{A}}}\rangle^{\log}_{B}}\rvert}}{\langle{{\lvert{\mathbb{A}}\rvert}}\rangle_{B}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\langle{{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}}\rangle^{\log}_{B})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 2.

This and Lemma
2.4
(with
t=2
) gives

III

\displaystyle\leq 2\,c(n,\Lambda)\,\lvert\log\mathbb{A}|_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}.

Collecting the estimates proves the claim.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

We shown that if
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}
is small enough, then it controls
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}(B)}
and therefore also

{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}_{\mu}(B)}
. On the other hand we know from Lemma
4.2
that
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}
is directly controlled by
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}(B)}
.
Based on standard John-Nirenberg estimates, it is possible to show that sufficient smallness of
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}_{\mu}(B)}
implies that
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}(B)}
can be linearly controlled by
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}_{\mu}(B)}
.

So overall, once one of the three quantities
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}
,
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}_{\mu}(B)}
and
{\lvert{{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}^{2}_{\mu}(B)}
is small, then they are all comparable. This allows to transfer results state in one language to the others. For example, smallness of
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}
implies the validity of the estimates in
[
CMP18
]
and we obtain
{\lVert{\nabla u}\rVert}_{L^{q}(\mu\,dx)}\lesssim{\lVert{F}\rVert}_{L^{q}(\mu\,dx)}
for
q>p=2
. However, the smallness of
{\lvert{\log{\mathbb{A}}}\rvert}_{{\mathrm{BMO}}(B)}
depends (negative) exponentially on
q
, see the discussion in
[
BDGN21
, Remark 23]
.
